MARKET DIRECTION: DOWN — NIFTY in a downtrend since Wed, 2 Sept 2026. Puts on the indices and the falling stocks — 178 on 33 names (4 indices, 29 stocks). Buys only on the stocks rising on their own — 11 found (COALINDIA, CYIENT, DRREDDY, JSL, KOTAKBANK, LALPATHLAB, LAURUSLABS, MANKIND, MOTILALOFS, PHOENIXLTD, SUNTV): 11 on FUTURES, 7 with calls.
NIFTY closed at 22,555.75 on Mon, 5 Oct 2026; its 20-day average 23,158.08, its 50-day 23,843.82, its 200-day 24,339.75. A close under the 20-day average, with the 20-day under the 50-day, is what the desk’s trend reader (market-regime.js) calls a downtrend. It is read from completed daily closes, so it does not change during the day. Puts are looked for on NIFTY, BANKNIFTY, FINNIFTY, MIDCPNIFTY, NIFTYNXT50, and on the 57 stocks the desk rates SELL or REDUCE that are falling themselves (under their own 20-day and 50-day averages); 19 more are rated SELL or REDUCE but are not falling, so no put. 33 of those names had contracts that cleared every gate (indices: NIFTY, BANKNIFTY, FINNIFTY, MIDCPNIFTY). Tested first, and it FAILED: on five years of NSE’s own NIFTY data, selling NIFTY in these downtrends made +0.29% a trade in the final year — 18 trades, t = 0.205, no different from nothing (the test was fixed before it was run). These puts are here because you asked for the market’s direction, not because they are proven. Rising against the market: every build reads each stock’s own trend; a stock over its own 20-day average, with that over its 50-day, gets futures and calls even while NIFTY falls (not if the desk rates it SELL or REDUCE). Tested first, and it FAILED: on NSE’s own files 2020–2026 (the 200 most traded stocks, this desk’s buy-limit, target and stop, after charges), buying them made −0.444% a trade in the final year — 1680 trades, 26.7% winners where 37.5% are needed to break even. Shorting the falling stocks in a downtrend also failed (−0.123% a trade). They are shown because you asked to see them, not because they work. Each row is tracked on TRACK from the moment it is shown.

Futures — 20 units each (GM Global's minimum)

If you take every row here (20 units each, before GM's charges): ₹2,77,262 in total; if every stop is hit, −₹4,159 — more if prices gap through the stops.
Most rows are in: Healthcare 4, Financial Services 2, Realty 1. Rows in one sector tend to fall together. With no trend, a row's target comes before its stop at most as often as its break-even (about 37.5%) — a price with no trend is a fair game — so before GM's charges these rows make money only if the BUY signal works. It was tested and FAILED: the desk’s own rating, replayed day by day on six years of NSE’s own prices (2,36,746 stock-days), bought on this tab’s limit, target and stop, made −0.028% a trade after charges, 36.7% winners where 37.5% are needed (32,725 trades, t = −0.44).

Results dates are NSE's own board-meeting filings, read 2026-10-06: 148 results meetings filed for the next 60 days. Companies must file at least two working days before the meeting (SEBI LODR Regulation 29), so “not announced” does not mean “no results”. Calls — you BUY the call, it pays if the stock RISES

If you take every row here (one lot each): ₹9,45,363 in total; if every stop is hit, −₹4,85,817 — more if prices gap through the stops.
These 43 rows are on 7 stocks: several strikes of one stock are one bet, not several. Most rows are in: Healthcare 24, Financial Services 12, Oil Gas & Consumable Fuels 6. Rows in one sector tend to fall together. With no trend and fairly priced options, a call is expected to lose about its charges: about −₹17,556.19 across all 43 rows, one lot each. 32 of 43 are priced above the stock’s own recent movement (implied above realised), which makes it worse. They make money only if the BUY signal works, and it failed: the desk’s own rating, replayed on six years of NSE’s prices, made −0.028% a trade on its BUY and ACCUMULATE rows (32,725 trades, 36.7% winners).

For calls these figures are the pessimistic end. Each call is repriced with Black-76 at its own implied volatility, held where it is — and in a crash that volatility jumps, which cushions a call (India VIX moved +66% on Mon, 7 Apr 2025). By how much depends on each stock’s own option volatility, which this page has no history for.

Puts — you BUY the put, it pays if the index or stock FALLS

If you take every row here (one lot each): ₹35,17,675 in total; if every stop is hit, −₹18,11,759 — more if prices gap through the stops.
These 178 rows are on 33 names: several strikes of one name are one bet, not several. Most rows are in: Index 55, Financial Services 30, Power 16. Rows in one sector tend to fall together. With no trend and fairly priced options, a put is expected to lose about its charges: about −₹70,000.24 across all 178 rows, one lot each. 142 of 178 are priced above their own recent movement (implied above realised), which makes it worse. They make money only if the fall continues, and selling NIFTY in these downtrends failed its pre-registered test (final year t = 0.205).
BAD-DAY RISK, every row together — 1 day in 20: −₹10,45,478 or worse · worst day: −₹23,07,572 · market opens 5% higher: −₹26,54,389
Today’s 178 rows (one lot each) replayed through each of the last 497 trading days, Tue, 8 Oct 2024 to Thu, 1 Oct 2026, on real prices, with every stop and target in the market. Not a forecast: these are days that already happened. Before charges.
1 day in 20: on the worst 25 of those 497 days, all the rows together lost at least ₹10,45,478 — ₹13,62,312 on average.
Worst day, Tue, 3 Feb 2026 (NIFTY +2.55%): 33 of 33 names rose and 166 stops were hit, 139 of them by a gap at the open — a loss of −₹23,07,572, against −₹17,58,431 if every stop had filled at its own price (before charges).
Market opens 5% higher: −₹26,54,389. Each name opens higher by its own measured sensitivity to NIFTY (median 1.18), and a stop fills at the open, not at its own price.
Together: on those 25 worst days, 92.9% of the names rose at once. These rows are closer to one bet on the market than to 33 separate ones.
Most of the bad-day loss: BANKNIFTY 18%, MIDCPNIFTY 12.9%, NIFTY 5.6%, FINNIFTY 4.8%, IDFCFIRSTB 4.3%.
Demergers, bonuses and splits are read from NSE’s own filings (Tue, 6 Oct 2026), so none of them counts as a crash.
For puts these figures are the OPTIMISTIC end. Each put is repriced with Black-76 at its own implied volatility, held where it is — but a put loses on the days the market rises, and on those days implied volatility usually falls, which takes still more off a put (India VIX moved −7% on Tue, 3 Feb 2026). The real loss on such a day can be larger than shown.
The stock puts lean on the desk’s SELL and REDUCE ratings, and those were tested: shorting them LOST. Replayed on six years of NSE’s prices, shorting every SELL/REDUCE row on this desk’s limit, target and stop made −0.139% a trade (35,236 trades, t = −3.31 — reliably negative), and over 20 sessions those stocks did slightly BETTER than the market. They are shown because you asked for the falling names, not because the rating picks fallers.
Looked for on NIFTY, BANKNIFTY, FINNIFTY, MIDCPNIFTY, NIFTYNXT50 and on the 57 stocks rated SELL or REDUCE that are falling themselves; 33 had contracts that cleared every gate, the indices first. One side per name, the side the market’s direction points to.

INDUSTRY-ADJUSTED REVERSAL (long-short futures) — Every 5th session: buy the 10% of stocks that lagged their industry group most over the last 5 sessions, short the 10% that led it most; close both 5 sessions later. Industry = NSE's own filings, which give none for about a third of the 200 stocks; those are compared with each other as one group, exactly as in the backtest. Backtest: +0.15% a rebalance, t 1.98, 248 rebalances over 60 months — just short of the bar. COLLECTING — 0 finished (a verdict needs 26 rebalances).
Next session is a rebalance. Short (led it): BHEL, DMART*, TFCILTD, INDIGO*, SYRMA*, HDFCLIFE*, POWERINDIA*, COFORGE*, AEGISLOG*, AXISBANK, ENRIN*, RATNAVEER*, AZAD*, MOREPENLAB. * = NSE gives no industry (18 of 28 names): compared with all such stocks together, as in the backtest.

Hit rate counts only trades that filled and then finished. IF RANDOM is how often the target would come before the stop if prices moved at random — each trade's break-even, before charges. Only a hit rate above it, over enough separate bets, is evidence of an edge. The touch odds the desk showed ignore the stop, so they always run higher. Futures returns are GROSS per unit; options are NET of every charge per lot. ALL has no average return (futures move about 1.5%, options about 50%), and its money is options net a lot plus futures at 20 units, gross.

HOW THIS IS SCORED. Every price this page freezes is bought on paper at the moment it is shown. Each build then checks the market, using the day’s high and low so a move between two builds is not missed. Every ambiguity counts against the record: a target and a stop in the same window is a stop; a target in the same window as the fill is not counted; a gap through the stop exits at the open; a fill is always at the limit, never better; a price from before the level was shown can never fill it. Unfilled day orders die at 15:30. Futures are held at most 20 sessions; options until expiry. Stored in the private repository under docs/ledger/, committed on every build, and copied to the Google Sheet.
WHERE THESE PRICES COME FROM. 221 priced from a live broker book, 0 from the 2026-10-05 settlement file. 221 of 221 options priced against the live futures price. Checked against NSE's own site at 2026-10-06 10:36 IST: 16 of 16 sampled contracts are the same contract on both (the same previous close), last prices within 2.88%. Broker login OK from the build server at 2026-10-06 10:34 IST — a NIFTY quote came back. F&O ban for 2026-10-06: AMBUJACEM, BANDHANBNK, SAIL — left out entirely, because NSE allows only exits in a banned stock. This page is a snapshot, rebuilt on a schedule — it is not a live feed. Check the price on your broker before placing anything. Net figures are after brokerage, STT at 0.15%, exchange, stamp and GST; the spread is extra and is only known where a live book was seen.
NO SHOCK SHIELD. Nothing on this page stops buys on a bad global morning — not a GIFT Nifty gap, a crude or S&P move, or an India VIX spike. Those signals were tested on NSE’s own data and failed (or had too few events to test), so they do not gate anything. Check the market yourself before acting on a shock day.
WHAT THIS DOES NOT ESTABLISH. That any of it makes money. The paper ledger has 926 resolved trade(s) so far — far too few for a measured hit rate — and every test fixed in advance so far, on data never used to build the rule, found no edge: the desk's own rating (replayed on six years of NSE data: −0.03% a trade on its buys, and its SELL/REDUCE shorts LOST −0.14% a trade, t = −3.3), 12-1 momentum, time-series momentum, low volatility, a 10-month market trend filter, and the NIFTY trend reader that now chooses between buys and puts (selling NIFTY in its downtrends: final year t = 0.21) — all six FAIL. The ranking is built on things that were measured, which is not the same as an edge that was proven. Size accordingly.
